# AU — Australia sights: individual worldwide reviews

- [Worldwide v2](../../../rules.md) · [Future-country workflow](../../future-country-workflow.md)
- Initial non-exhaustive inventory. UNESCO [country inventory](https://whc.unesco.org/en/statesparties/AU/) checked 2026-11-25: 21 inscribed and 8 tentative properties, all represented at property level. Individual serial components are not comprehensive. Sources linked for context; provisional editorial scores, no fully fact-checked record claimed. Several points are deliberately approximate and require corroboration. Peer comparisons qualitative, not a claim of checking current peer YAML scores. No current visitor access or safety guarantee.

- Current decisions use one canonical heading per destination ID; earlier text and sources are preserved in [review history](history-evidence-followup-2026-11-25.md).

## sight-au-great-barrier-reef — Great Barrier Reef — 2026-11-25

- YAML: [source](../../../countries/AU/sights/great-barrier-reef.yaml)
- Action: new
- Precise subject: Whole inscribed reef system
- Location: representative -18.2861, 147.7; not an entrance. See uncertainty below.
- Evidence status: source-linked-unverified
- Limited factual references (2026-11-25; general context and property description, not exact score evidence):
  - https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/154/
  - https://www.dcceew.gov.au/parks-heritage/heritage/places/world-heritage-list
  - https://www.australia.com/en/things-to-do/nature-and-national-parks/australias-top-natural-attractions.html
- Missing or uncertain facts: Whole inscribed reef system; representative offshore approximate point, not a dive location. High fields reflect reef-system scale, ecological diversity and varied visitor environments, not guaranteed coral condition; conservation threats documented by UNESCO. Coordinates other than explicitly sourced ones are approximate and need independent verification.

- 2026-11-26 evidence follow-up: Reef Authority 2025–26 annual monitoring summary https://www.gbrmpa.gov.au/news/aims-long-term-monitoring-program-annual-summary-report-2025-26-released reports early recovery after widespread bleaching, while https://www.gbrmpa.gov.au/learn/reef-health/reef-snapshot records regional northern bleaching and localized impacts during the 2025–26 summer. Retain normal-condition system rating 96.80 provisionally; do not imply uniform current coral health or guaranteed encounters.

### Worldwide peer comparisons
- Raja Ampat (Indonesia): exceptional reef biodiversity and islands
- Tubbataha Reefs (Philippines): concentrated protected reef diving
- Marovo Lagoon (Solomon Islands): distinctive double-barrier lagoon
- Rock Islands Southern Lagoon (Palau): more concentrated limestone-island scenery

#### Current YAML benchmark calibration (2026-11-26)

Core values I/U/S/E/D from worldwide YAML; original qualitative comparisons retained. Editorial calibration, not independent factual verification.

- [raja ampat archipelago (ID)](../../../countries/ID/sights/raja-ampat-archipelago.yaml) — 9.8/9.9/9.5/9.7/9.8; World-leading reef-island biodiversity and visitor diversity, slightly greater island relief.
- [tubbataha (PH)](../../../countries/PH/sights/tubbataha.yaml) — 9.3/9.2/9.3/9.1/9.0; More concentrated protected open-ocean reef experience, narrower system.
- [sipadan island reefs (MY)](../../../countries/MY/sights/sipadan-island-reefs.yaml) — 9.5/9.6/9.1/9.5/8.9; More focused exceptional dive encounters, less system breadth.
- [rock islands southern lagoon (PW)](../../../countries/PW/sights/rock-islands-southern-lagoon.yaml) — 9.5/9.5/9.3/9.4/9.3; More compact limestone-island and reef ensemble, less coral system scale.

High-score check: Raja Ampat impact 9.8, uniqueness 9.9, significance 9.5, depth 9.8, nature/wildlife/adventure/photography all 9.9; Aldabra significance and wildlife 9.8; Sipadan nature/wildlife/adventure 9.7; Rock Islands photography 9.5. GBR's 9.7 impact, 9.8 uniqueness, 9.7 significance, 9.7 depth, 9.8 nature/wildlife and 9.5 adventure/photography are supported by interconnected system scale, not every individual reef or guaranteed encounter. 96.80 remains provisional.

### Worldwide peer comparisons
- Nan Madol (Micronesia): monumental ceremonial architecture
- Stonehenge (United Kingdom): iconic prehistoric monument
- Tsodilo (Botswana): concentrated sacred rock-art landscape and interpreted walking routes; Kakadu adds a much wider wetland and escarpment experience (https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/1021/; https://www.botswanatourism.co.bw/explore/tsodilo-hills)
- Angkor (Cambodia): greater monumental built landscape

#### Current YAML benchmark calibration (2026-11-26)

Core values I/U/S/E/D from worldwide YAML; original qualitative comparisons retained. Editorial calibration, not independent factual verification.

- [tsodilo hills (BW)](../../../countries/BW/sights/tsodilo-hills.yaml) — 8.5/9.3/9.6/8.2/8.2; Greater focused rock-art significance, narrower natural visitor breadth.
- [twyfelfontein (NA)](../../../countries/NA/sights/twyfelfontein.yaml) — 9.0/9.4/9.5/8.8/8.5; Comparable significant rock-art landscape, narrower ecosystem and visitor scope.
- [zuojiang huashan rock art (CN)](../../../countries/CN/sights/zuojiang-huashan-rock-art.yaml) — 9.1/9.6/9.4/8.6/8.4; Exceptional river-cliff rock art, narrower cultural landscape.
- [shiretoko national park (JP)](../../../countries/JP/sights/shiretoko-national-park.yaml) — 9.5/9.5/9.4/9.1/9.4; Comparable protected landscape breadth and ecology, lacks equivalent rock-art heritage.

High-score check: Tsodilo significance 9.6, history 9.7, culture 9.6; Twyfelfontein significance 9.5, history 9.6, culture 9.4; Huashan history 9.7, culture 9.6. Kakadu's significance/history/culture 9.5 are supported by long living cultural continuity and broad mixed heritage; avoid transferring one gallery to the whole park.

## 2026-11-25 — additional evidence and comparator corrections

This supplement preserves the prior individual decisions; no YAML ratings changed in this pass. Sources below were actually consulted on 2026-11-25. These findings corroborate specific facts, not all facts or subjective scores for any record.

- **sight-au-kakadu-national-park:** corrected an invalid self-comparison in the original peer list to Tsodilo. UNESCO documents Kakadu's long Indigenous occupation, rock art, wetlands and sandstone escarpment (https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/147/). Parks Australia documents the Ubirr rock-art walk and Burrungkuy's varied public routes (https://kakadu.gov.au/things-do/activities/walks/ubirr-walk/; https://kakadu.gov.au/discover/regions/burrungkuy-nourlangie/). Tsodilo is a genuine living sacred landscape with concentrated galleries and public trails (sources in corrected peer list). Kakadu's 9.5 significance/history/culture remains a provisional editorial judgment of the whole landscape, not its individual galleries. Numerical calibration against current global YAML peers remains outstanding.
- **sight-au-cape-york-cultural-landscapes:** Queensland explicitly confirms the tentative submission has no defined boundary; its representative points reflect consent to the submission, not general visitor permission (https://environment.qld.gov.au/management/world-heritage-areas/potential/cape-york). The state separately confirms public Split Rock and guided Quinkan visits (https://www.qld.gov.au/environment/plants-animals/conservation/community/land-sea-rangers/first-nations-tourism-experiences). Retain experience/depth as null for the aggregate proposal; assess Quinkan as its own visitor destination in a future inventory expansion. Do not infer public access from coordinates.
- **sight-au-gondwana-rainforests-extension:** UNESCO's tentative submission explicitly identifies Bunya Mountains National Park, parts of Richmond Range, Carrai Plateau and additional connectivity areas; proposed total is 689,364 hectares (https://whc.unesco.org/en/tentativelists/5541/). Queensland documents 35 km of tracks in Bunya Mountains (https://parks.qld.gov.au/parks/bunya-mountains/things-to-do). NSW documents Richmond Range walks but also describes them as World Heritage rainforest, so they cannot automatically be attributed to proposed additions (https://www.nationalparks.nsw.gov.au/visit-a-park/parks/richmond-range-national-park). Retain null experience/depth for aggregate additions until visitor routes and extension boundaries can be matched; Bunya Mountains itself is a candidate separate sight.
- **sight-au-heard-mcdonald-islands:** Australian Antarctic Division states the 2014–2024 plan expired and has no legal effect (https://www.antarctica.gov.au/antarctic-operations/stations-and-field-locations/heard-island/protection-and-management/management-plan/). Transitional arrangements require assessment and authorisation of almost all activities (https://www.antarctica.gov.au/antarctic-operations/stations-and-field-locations/heard-island/protection-and-management/management-plan/transitional-management-arrangements/). A replacement draft was announced on 28 September 2026 (https://www.antarctica.gov.au/news/2026/public-consultation-opens-on-draft-marine-reserve-management-plan-for-heard-island-and-mcdonald-islands/); current tourism FAQ and historical permit do not establish a current ordinary visitor offer. Retain null experience/depth and do not use expired visitor-zone rules as current authority. Remoteness alone is not a score deduction.
- **sight-au-great-sandy-world-heritage-area:** independently corroborated tentative extension scope and distinction from K’gari via UNESCO and Queensland government (https://whc.unesco.org/en/tentativelists/5480/; https://environment.qld.gov.au/management/world-heritage-areas/potential/faq). Keep 88.25 provisional and exclude inscribed K’gari and restricted military land from credited visitor experience; check actual route/zoning overlap before full fact-checked status.
- **sight-au-workers-assembly-halls:** UNESCO's tentative listing and Heritage Victoria's 2026 nomination notice confirm the two Australian halls and transnational scope (https://whc.unesco.org/en/tentativelists/6698/; https://www.heritage.vic.gov.au/about-us/news/news-stories/melbournes-victorian-trades-hall-nominated-for-world-heritage-list). Keep 83.55 provisional; current interior access and representative GPS still need checks.


### Heritage inventory recheck (2026-11-26)

UNESCO country inventory https://whc.unesco.org/en/statesparties/au/ confirms 21 inscribed and eight tentative properties; existing property-level coverage retained. Serial components and ordinary visitor access remain incompletely verified.

### Pass 206 scope decisions

Whyalla giant Australian cuttlefish aggregation deferred for physical-condition reassessment: SA government reports 1,811 in 2026, lowest on record, ongoing investigation including possible algal bloom effects (https://www.environment.sa.gov.au/news-hub/news/articles/2026/09/more-monitoring-and-research-for-giant-australian-cuttlefish-as-numbers-reach-historic-lows). This is not merely a temporary visitor closure. Christmas Island red crab migration belongs to CX territory queue (https://parksaustralia.gov.au/news/millions-of-crabs-march-on-christmas-island/), not mainland AU. Mount Field confirmed within Tasmanian Wilderness (https://parks.tas.gov.au/explore-our-parks/mount-field-national-park/mount-field-history), specific day-walk scope only. Otways excludes coastal drive; Gordon cruise includes Sarah Island, excludes Franklin rafting. Ball's Pyramid already within Lord Howe island group scope, no duplicate pending specialist experience review. Lady Elliot still deferred; no existing score changes.
